Compared: the terms this corpus defines more than one way

This corpus defines 531 terms more than one way. For 50 there are enough attested uses to say how much of its own usage any one definition accounts for; for 14 of those, no definition accounts for half. 39 more terms are defined several ways over and nothing covers anything at all.

the showcase · the corpus · concepts · search

Two levels, two questions

A definition can be tested two ways, and this project publishes both. They are not two grades of the same measurement: they answer different questions, they are measured against different evidence, and they are never put in one column.

Scored

Which definition sorts things in and out correctly?

evidence
an adjudicated case: a thing, a verdict, a verbatim quote, a reason
needs
rival definitions, about 400 adjudicated cases, and a reviewed criteria layer
reports
Matthews correlation, against adjudicated cases, printed as MCC 0.747
control
a circular definition scores near 1 and is disqualified by gate; it calibrates the table rather than winning it
terms today
1  art

Compared

Which definition matches how this corpus uses the term?

evidence
an attested use: a gloss written by a paper, and the sentence licensing it
needs
two or more rival definitions, and four or more attested uses before a number is printed
reports
share of this corpus's attested uses the definition accounts for, printed as 50% of uses
control
a gloss borrowed from an unrelated term must lose; if it does not, the term reports no separation rather than a winner
terms today
89 with a number, 438 shown without one
Compared, not judged: this is how much of the corpus's own attested use each definition accounts for, not whether it sorts things in and out.

Compared 50

Least covered first. A low figure is the finding, not a gap: it means no single definition in this corpus accounts for how the corpus itself uses the word. Each definition is shown with the paper that wrote it and the sentence that licensed it.

priming

4 rival definitions · 6 attested uses · 4 papers · no definition accounts for any other · decoy control reached 0% of uses

0% of uses

Priming, the fourth modality of non-declarative or implicit memory, is an effect whereby exposure to certain stimuli influences the response given to stimuli presented later. An example is in order. If you present a list of words to a person that contains the word ‘ball,’ and then the person is asked to participate in a task to complete words, they are more likely to respond with the word ball to the presentation of the word bowl than if they had not previously seen that word in the original list. Thus, the priming capacity can affect the choice of a particular word on a test to complete words, even long after conscious recollection of the primed words has been forgotten.

error correction

2 rival definitions · 2 attested uses · 1 papers · too few attested uses to measure coverage

—

The brain propagates neuronal signals accurately and rapidly. Nevertheless, whether and how a pool of cortical neurons transmits an undistorted message to a target remains unclear. We apply optogenetic white noise signals to small assemblies of cortical pyramidal cells (PYRs) in freely moving mice. The directly activated PYRs exhibit a spike timing precision of several milliseconds. Instead of losing precision, interneurons driven via synaptic activation exhibit higher precision with respect to the white noise signal. Compared with directly activated PYRs, postsynaptic interneuron spike trains allow better signal reconstruction, demonstrating error correction.

Levi2022-error-correction-and-improved-precision-of-s

—

Thus, the WN signal applied to the PYRs is reconstructed better based on the postsynaptic compared with the presynaptic spike trains. We term this phenomenon “error correction.”

a structure or plan of descriptive categories and relations presumed to account for a phenomenon

A framework usually denotes a structure, overview, outline, system or plan consisting of various descriptive categories, e.g. concepts, constructs or variables, and the relations between them that are presumed to account for a phenomenon [ 38 ]. Frameworks do not provide explanations; they only describe empirical phenomena by fitting them into a set of categories [ 29 ].

phase precession

2 rival definitions · 2 attested uses · 2 papers · too few attested uses to measure coverage

—

There is a systematic change in the phase of theta oscillations at which a place cell fires as the animal moves through its firing field (“phase precession”; O’Keefe and Recce, 1993 in rodents; Qasim et al., 2021 in humans).

Billig2022-the-hearing-hippocampus

—

Theta phase precession. The time at which a cell fires relative to the ongoing theta cycle (“phase”) decreases as the animal moves through space. Thus, the cell initially fires at the end of a theta cycle, then slightly before the end of the next cycle, until it eventually fires its last spikes near the beginning of the ongoing theta cycle. This phenomenon is called “phase precession.” Initially described in place cells, phase precession has also been reported in other hippocampal cells, including interneurons, “episode,” and sound-responsive cells (in which case the movement is considered in a more abstract space), as well as in other brain structures such as the medial prefrontal cortex or the ventral striatum.

Drieu2019-hippocampal-sequences-during-exploration-mec

—

“Sensory memory is the capacity for briefly retaining the large amounts of information that people encounter daily” ( Siegler and Alibali, 2005 ). There are three types of sensory memory: echoic memory, iconic memory, and haptic memory. Iconic memory retains information that is gathered through sight, echoic memory retains information gathered through auditory stimuli and haptic memory retains data acquired through touch.

theta sequence

2 rival definitions · 3 attested uses · 1 papers · too few attested uses to measure coverage

—

Theta sequence. Sequential activation of hippocampal cell assemblies paced by the ongoing theta rhythm (~8 Hz) and representing the ongoing trajectory at a highly accelerated rate. For instance, when a rat successively traverses place fields A, B, and C (which lasts on the order of a second), in the overlapping region, ongoing theta cycles contain spikes A, B, and C in the same order (within only ~150 ms). Theta sequences typically span one theta cycle, but longer sequences have also been described.

Drieu2019-hippocampal-sequences-during-exploration-mec

How this was measured

A definition accounts for a use when at least a third of the definition's content words, and at least two of them, appear in that use's own statement — its gloss plus the verbatim sentence licensing it. Normalising by the definition's own length keeps a four-word gloss and a thirty-word gloss comparable. A definition never gets credit for covering itself, and the term's own words are dropped, since every definition of bias contains the word bias.

Lexical overlap will always produce a number, so every term is also run against four decoy definitions taken from unrelated terms, drawn deterministically so two builds give the same control. If the best real definition does not beat the best decoy, no winner is published. Across all 531 terms the mean decoy reached 1% of uses.

A definition is credited only for uses in other papers. The corpus records a definition in up to three layers, so one paper often arrives as two or three rows with reworded glosses; those collapse into one definition, and a paper restating itself earns nothing. Before that rule, 1,191 terms looked as though they carried rival definitions. 531 of them actually do.

4 terms reached no separation from the decoy control and is not reported: outcome, recall, reliability, sample size.

Generated by TOOLS/build_compare_page.py from PLATFORM/DATA/compare_index.json; the two levels are defined once in TOOLS/scoring_levels.py. Design from REPORTS/report54.html.
